The boot lip is 711mm high, and there is useful underfloor space around the spare wheel for accessories or emergency equipment.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e31:Tc2c,\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe Creta\u0026rsquo;s cabin is one of its strongest areas. The facelift brought a beige-and-grey dashboard theme, twin 10.25-inch screens and a more premium-looking layout. The screens are integrated into the dashboard, so they do not block the forward view.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe digital instrument cluster offers multiple themes and can also show blind-spot camera feeds when the turn indicators are used. The touchscreen is crisp and quick to respond, and the 360-degree camera feed is clear. This makes the camera system useful not only while parking, but also while judging space in dense traffic.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe cabin offers a good mix of digital features and physical controls. The dual-zone climate-control panel, drive mode selector, electronic parking brake, auto-hold switch, 360-camera button and seat ventilation controls are easy to access. The front storage spaces are also practical, with door bottle holders, a deep front armrest storage area, cup holders, a wireless charging pad and a cooled glovebox.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe front seats are well cushioned and supportive. The driving position is confidence-inspiring because of the low-set dashboard and good forward visibility. Some details could still be better. The front centre armrest could have been placed higher and further forward, and the top variants still miss small features like a driver vanity mirror, a passenger vanity mirror light, multi-colour ambient lighting and an adjustable rear middle headrest.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eRear-seat space is good. With the front seat set for a 5ft 7in to 5ft 8in driver, there is more than adequate kneeroom and legroom. There is also plenty of space under the front seats to slide your feet. The panoramic sunroof, light headliner and large windows make the rear cabin feel airy.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe rear bench is most comfortable for 2 adults. A third passenger can sit in the middle, and all 3 rear occupants get 3-point seatbelts. However, the middle backrest is not very comfortable because it also works as the fold-down armrest, and there is no adjustable middle headrest. The rear seat gets 2-step recline, sunshades, AC vents, a storage cubby and 2 USB-C charging ports.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e32:Td79,\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe 1.5L normal petrol-CVT is the easiest Creta powertrain to recommend for most city users. It is not the most powerful option, but it is smooth, quiet and responsive enough for regular use. The CVT does not feel laggy in normal driving, and the auto-hold function makes stop-go traffic easier.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eIn V3Cars\u0026rsquo; test, the Creta 1.5L normal petrol-CVT returned 11.16kmpl in the city and 15.77kmpl on the highway. At 100kmph, the engine runs at around 2000rpm, and NVH levels remain well controlled. The main limitation is highway performance with a full load. With 5 occupants and luggage, this powertrain may feel adequate rather than quick.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe 1.5L turbo petrol-DCT is the performance pick. It is punchy, responsive and smooth on the highway. In V3Cars\u0026rsquo; test, the Creta turbo-DCT returned 10.85kmpl in the city and 18.89kmpl on the highway. At 100kmph in 7th gear, the engine runs at around 1800rpm, which helps it cruise quietly.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe DCT did not show overheating or lag during V3Cars\u0026rsquo; test in Delhi traffic and April heat. However, buyers who spend a lot of time in heavy stop-go traffic should still remember that Hyundai recommends earlier DCT oil changes in such use cases.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe diesel engine is the sensible option for buyers with higher running, frequent highway use or regular full-load travel. Its 250Nm torque output should make it better suited to long-distance driving than the normal petrol. The diesel-TC automatic is also the better pick if you want a relaxed long-distance Creta without choosing the turbo petrol-DCT.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eRide comfort is tuned well for everyday use. On broken roads, the Creta feels comfortable, though the soft suspension allows some body roll. The Creta\u0026rsquo;s visibility, light steering and smooth automatics make it easy to drive in the city. On the highway, the turbo-DCT feels strong, but the high-speed braking feel could be more reassuring.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe ADAS suite worked well in V3Cars\u0026rsquo; test. Forward collision warning and mitigation, adaptive cruise control with stop-and-go, rear cross-traffic alert, rear collision avoidance, door-open warning, blind-spot warning and blind-view camera all worked as intended. These features are useful, but they should still be treated as driver assistance, not a replacement for attention.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e33:Ta84,\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eThe Hyundai Creta remains a strong all-round C-segment SUV because it does most things well. It is comfortable, refined, feature-rich, easy to drive and available with petrol, turbo petrol and diesel engine options. The facelift also fixed the earlier model\u0026rsquo;s polarising design and gave the cabin a more premium feel.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eIts weak points are mostly related to price and feature distribution. There is no Creta variant under Rs. 10 lakh now, the EX and S variants do not offer strong value, adaptive cruise control is not available with manual transmission, and the turbo petrol engine is not available with a manual transmission. The Creta also does not offer a strong hybrid option, which some rivals do.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eFor 1.5L normal petrol-manual buyers, the S(O) is our top recommendation because it offers the best balance of features and value. The E is suitable only for strict budget buyers who are open to aftermarket personalisation. The EX and EX(O) variants are expensive upgrades and best avoided. The SX Premium is for feel-good features without ADAS, while the King is the best pick if you want ADAS tech.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eFor 1.5L normal petrol-CVT buyers, the S(O) is again our top recommendation. The EX(O) is better skipped in favour of the S(O). The SX Premium is the budget-friendly way to get ADAS tech with the automatic, while the King variants are worth considering if you want the fuller feature package.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\r\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-size:11pt\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:Arial,sans-serif\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color:#000000\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-weight:400\"\u003eFor turbo petrol-DCT buyers, the King and King dual-tone variants are both worth considering because Hyundai offer limited variant choice with this powertrain. For diesel-manual buyers, the S(O) is our top recommendation, while the King is the better choice if ADAS is a priority. For diesel-TC automatic buyers, the S(O) is the top recommendation, while the King variants are for buyers who want more features and are comfortable with the higher
